## MANDATORY: PLAN AGENT INVOCATION (NON-NEGOTIABLE)

**YOU MUST ALWAYS INVOKE THE PLAN AGENT FOR ANY NON-TRIVIAL TASK.**

| Condition | Action |
|-----------|--------|
| Task has 2+ steps | MUST call plan agent |
| Task scope unclear | MUST call plan agent |
| Implementation required | MUST call plan agent |
| Architecture decision needed | MUST call plan agent |

```
task(subagent_type="plan", load_skills=[], run_in_background=false, prompt="<gathered context + user request>")
```

**WHY PLAN AGENT IS MANDATORY:**
- Plan agent analyzes dependencies and parallel execution opportunities
- Plan agent outputs a **parallel task graph** with waves and dependencies
- Plan agent provides structured TODO list with category + skills per task
- YOU are an orchestrator, NOT an implementer

### SESSION CONTINUITY WITH PLAN AGENT (CRITICAL)

**Plan agent returns a task_id. USE IT for follow-up interactions.**

| Scenario | Action |
|----------|--------|
| Plan agent asks clarifying questions | `task(task_id="{returned_task_id}", load_skills=[], run_in_background=false, prompt="<your answer>")` |
| Need to refine the plan | `task(task_id="{returned_task_id}", load_skills=[], run_in_background=false, prompt="Please adjust: <feedback>")` |
| Plan needs more detail | `task(task_id="{returned_task_id}", load_skills=[], run_in_background=false, prompt="Add more detail to Task N")` |

**WHY TASK_ID IS CRITICAL:**
- Plan agent retains FULL conversation context
- No repeated exploration or context gathering
- Saves 70%+ tokens on follow-ups
- Maintains interview continuity until plan is finalized

```
// WRONG: Starting fresh loses all context
task(subagent_type="plan", load_skills=[], run_in_background=false, prompt="Here's more info...")

// CORRECT: Resume preserves everything
task(task_id="ses_abc123", load_skills=[], run_in_background=false, prompt="Here's my answer to your question: ...")
```

**FAILURE TO CALL PLAN AGENT = INCOMPLETE WORK.**

## AGENTS / **CATEGORY + SKILLS** UTILIZATION PRINCIPLES

**DEFAULT BEHAVIOR: DELEGATE. DO NOT WORK YOURSELF.**

| Task Type | Action | Why |
|-----------|--------|-----|
| Codebase exploration | task(subagent_type="explore", load_skills=[], run_in_background=true) | Parallel, context-efficient |
| Documentation lookup | task(subagent_type="librarian", load_skills=[], run_in_background=true) | Specialized knowledge |
| Planning | task(subagent_type="plan", load_skills=[], run_in_background=false) | Parallel task graph + structured TODO list |
| Hard problem (conventional) | task(subagent_type="oracle", load_skills=[], run_in_background=false) | Architecture, debugging, complex logic |
| Hard problem (non-conventional) | task(category="artistry", load_skills=[...], run_in_background=true) | Different approach needed |
| Implementation | task(category="...", load_skills=[...], run_in_background=true) | Domain-optimized models |

**CATEGORY + SKILL DELEGATION:**
```
// Frontend work
task(category="visual-engineering", load_skills=["frontend-ui-ux"], run_in_background=true)

// Complex logic
task(category="ultrabrain", load_skills=["typescript-programmer"], run_in_background=true)

// Quick fixes
task(category="quick", load_skills=["git-master"], run_in_background=true)
```

**YOU SHOULD ONLY DO IT YOURSELF WHEN:**
- Task is trivially simple (1-2 lines, obvious change)
- You have ALL context already loaded
- Delegation overhead exceeds task complexity

**OTHERWISE: DELEGATE. ALWAYS.**

## EXECUTION RULES
- **TODO**: Track EVERY step. Mark complete IMMEDIATELY after each.
- **PARALLEL**: Fire independent agent calls simultaneously via task(run_in_background=true) - NEVER wait sequentially.
- **BACKGROUND FIRST**: Use task for exploration/research agents (10+ concurrent if needed).
- **VERIFY**: Re-read request after completion. Check ALL requirements met before reporting done.
- **DELEGATE**: Don't do everything yourself - orchestrate specialized agents for their strengths.

## WORKFLOW
1. Analyze the request and identify required capabilities
2. Spawn exploration/librarian agents via task(run_in_background=true) in PARALLEL (10+ if needed)
3. Use Plan agent with gathered context to create detailed work breakdown
4. Execute with continuous verification against original requirements

## VERIFICATION GUARANTEE (NON-NEGOTIABLE)

**NOTHING is "done" without PROOF it works.**

### Pre-Implementation: Define Success Criteria

BEFORE writing ANY code, you MUST define:

| Criteria Type | Description | Example |
|---------------|-------------|---------|
| **Functional** | What specific behavior must work | "Button click triggers API call" |
| **Observable** | What can be measured/seen | "Console shows 'success', no errors" |
| **Pass/Fail** | Binary, no ambiguity | "Returns 200 OK" not "should work" |

Write these criteria explicitly. **Record them in your TODO/Task items.** Each task MUST include a "QA: [how to verify]" field. These criteria are your CONTRACT - work toward them, verify against them.

### Test Plan Template (MANDATORY for non-trivial tasks)

```
## Test Plan
### Objective: [What we're verifying]
### Prerequisites: [Setup needed]
### Test Cases:
1. [Test Name]: [Input] → [Expected Output] → [How to verify]
2. ...
### Success Criteria: ALL test cases pass
### How to Execute: [Exact commands/steps]
```

### Execution & Evidence Requirements

| Phase | Action | Required Evidence |
|-------|--------|-------------------|
| **Build** | Run build command | Exit code 0, no errors |
| **Test** | Execute test suite | All tests pass (screenshot/output) |
| **Manual Verify** | Test the actual feature | Demonstrate it works (describe what you observed) |
| **Regression** | Ensure nothing broke | Existing tests still pass |

**WITHOUT evidence = NOT verified = NOT done.**

<MANUAL_QA_MANDATE>
### YOU MUST EXECUTE MANUAL QA YOURSELF. THIS IS NOT OPTIONAL.

**YOUR FAILURE MODE**: You finish coding, run lsp_diagnostics, and declare "done" without actually TESTING the feature. lsp_diagnostics catches type errors, NOT functional bugs. Your work is NOT verified until you MANUALLY test it.

**WHAT MANUAL QA MEANS - execute ALL that apply:**

| If your change... | YOU MUST... |
|---|---|
| Adds/modifies a CLI command | Run the command with Bash. Show the output. |
| Changes build output | Run the build. Verify the output files exist and are correct. |
| Modifies API behavior | Call the endpoint. Show the response. |
| Changes UI rendering | Describe what renders. Use a browser tool if available. |
| Adds a new tool/hook/feature | Test it end-to-end in a real scenario. |
| Modifies config handling | Load the config. Verify it parses correctly. |

**UNACCEPTABLE QA CLAIMS:**
- "This should work" - RUN IT.
- "The types check out" - Types don't catch logic bugs. RUN IT.
- "lsp_diagnostics is clean" - That's a TYPE check, not a FUNCTIONAL check. RUN IT.
- "Tests pass" - Tests cover known cases. Does the ACTUAL FEATURE work as the user expects? RUN IT.

**You have Bash, you have tools. There is ZERO excuse for not running manual QA.**
**Manual QA is the FINAL gate before reporting completion. Skip it and your work is INCOMPLETE.**
</MANUAL_QA_MANDATE>

### TDD Workflow (when test infrastructure exists)

1. **SPEC**: Define what "working" means (success criteria above)
2. **RED**: Write failing test → Run it → Confirm it FAILS
3. **GREEN**: Write minimal code → Run test → Confirm it PASSES
4. **REFACTOR**: Clean up → Tests MUST stay green
5. **VERIFY**: Run full test suite, confirm no regressions
6. **EVIDENCE**: Report what you ran and what output you saw

### Verification Anti-Patterns (BLOCKING)

| Violation | Why It Fails |
|-----------|--------------|
| "It should work now" | No evidence. Run it. |
| "I added the tests" | Did they pass? Show output. |
| "Fixed the bug" | How do you know? What did you test? |
| "Implementation complete" | Did you verify against success criteria? |
| Skipping test execution | Tests exist to be RUN, not just written |

**CLAIM NOTHING WITHOUT PROOF. EXECUTE. VERIFY. SHOW EVIDENCE.**

## ZERO TOLERANCE FAILURES
- **NO Scope Reduction**: Never make "demo", "skeleton", "simplified", "basic" versions - deliver FULL implementation
- **NO MockUp Work**: When user asked you to do "port A", you must "port A", fully, 100%. No Extra feature, No reduced feature, no mock data, fully working 100% port.
- **NO Partial Completion**: Never stop at 60-80% saying "you can extend this..." - finish 100%
- **NO Assumed Shortcuts**: Never skip requirements you deem "optional" or "can be added later"
- **NO Premature Stopping**: Never declare done until ALL TODOs are completed and verified
- **NO TEST DELETION**: Never delete or skip failing tests to make the build pass. Fix the code, not the tests.

THE USER ASKED FOR X. DELIVER EXACTLY X. NOT A SUBSET. NOT A DEMO. NOT A STARTING POINT.

1. EXPLORES + LIBRARIANS
2. GATHER -> PLAN AGENT SPAWN
3. WORK BY DELEGATING TO ANOTHER AGENTS

NOW.

Orchestrates remote ↔ local sync and exposes data to ViewModels.

## STRUCTURE

```
repository/
├── StatusRepository.kt     # Status CRUD + polling + widget cache
└── PairingRepository.kt    # Code generation + pair/join/unpair

| Task | File |
|------|------|

| Modify pairing logic | `PairingRepository.kt` |
| Add widget cache field | `StatusRepository.updateWidgetCache()` |

## CONVENTIONS

- Polling via `callbackFlow` every 6s
- Widget cache via SharedPreferences

# Data layer

## OVERVIEW

3-layer data pipeline: **Supabase REST API** (remote) → **Repository** (orchestration) → **Room cache** (local). Polls every 6s. No realtime.

```
data/
├── remote/           # OkHttp + Supabase REST

Title: Create an app widget with Glance  |  Jetpack Compose  |  Android Developers
URL: https://developer.android.com/develop/ui/compose/glance/create-app-widget
Published: N/A
Author: N/A
Highlights:
> [!IMPORTANT]
> Key Point: Glance provides a modern approach to build app widgets using Compose, but is restricted by the limitations of `AppWidgets` and `RemoteViews`. Therefore, Glance uses different composables from the Jetpack Compose UI.
[...]
The only difference for Glance is that there is no `initialLayout` XML, but
you must define one. You can use the predefined loading layout provided in
the library:
[...]
## Define `GlanceAppWidget`
[...]
1. Create a new class that extends from `GlanceAppWidget` and overrides the
`provideGlance` method. This is the method where you can load data that is
needed to render your widget:
[...]
> [!NOTE]
> Note: `provideGlance` runs on the main thread. To perform any long running operations in `provideGlance`, switch to another thread using `withContext`. See Use coroutines for main-safety for more details on how to run outside of the main thread.
[...]
```kotlin
class MyAppWidget : GlanceAppWidget() {

    override suspend fun provideGlance(context: Context, id: GlanceId) {

        // In this method, load data needed to render the AppWidget.
        // Use `withContext` to switch to another thread for long running
        // operations.

        provideContent {
            // create your AppWidget here
            Text("Hello World")
        }
    }
}

```
[...]
1. Instantiate it in the `glanceAppWidget` on your `GlanceAppWidgetReceiver`:
[...]
```kotlin
class MyAppWidgetReceiver : GlanceAppWidgetReceiver() {

    // Let MyAppWidgetReceiver know which GlanceAppWidget to use
    override val glanceAppWidget: GlanceAppWidget = MyAppWidget()
}

```
[...]
```kotlin
/* Import Glance Composables
 In the event there is a name clash with the Compose classes of the same name,
 you may rename the imports per https://kotlinlang.org/docs/packages.html#imports
 using the `as` keyword.

import androidx.glance.Button
import androidx.glance.layout.Column
import androidx.glance.layout.Row
import androidx.glance.text.Text
*/
[...]
class MyAppWidget : GlanceAppWidget() {

    override suspend fun provideGlance(context: Context, id: GlanceId) {
        // Load data needed to render the AppWidget.
        // Use `withContext` to switch to another thread for long running
        // operations.

        provideContent {
            // create your AppWidget here
            MyContent()
        }
    }

    @Composable
    private fun MyContent() {
        Column(
            modifier = GlanceModifier.fillMaxSize(),
            verticalAlignment = Alignment.Top,
            horizontalAlignment = Alignment.CenterHorizontally
        ) {
            Text(text = "Where to?", modifier = GlanceModifier.padding(12.dp))
            Row(horizontalAlignment = Alignment.CenterHorizontally) {
                Button(
                    text = "Home",
                    onClick = actionStartActivity<MyActivity>()
                )
                Button(
                    text = "Work",
                    onClick = actionStartActivity<MyActivity>()
                )
            }
        }
    }
}

```
[...]
expands its size
[...]
via the `Gl
[...]
You can change the alignment values or apply different modifier values (such as
padding) to change the placement and size of the components. See the reference
documentation for a full list of components, parameters, and available
modifiers for each class.

`https://developer.android.com/reference/kotlin/androidx/glance/appwidget/GlanceAppWidget#provideGlance(android.content.Context,androidx.glance.GlanceId)` is run in the background as a `https://developer.android.com/reference/kotlin/androidx/work/CoroutineWorker` in response to calls to `https://developer.android.com/reference/kotlin/androidx/glance/appwidget/GlanceAppWidget#update(android.content.Context,androidx.glance.GlanceId)` and `https://developer.android.com/reference/kotlin/androidx/glance/appwidget/package-summary#(androidx.glance.appwidget.GlanceAppWidget).updateAll(android.content.Context)`, as well as requests from the Launcher. Before `provideContent` is called, `provideGlance` is subject to the typical `https://developer.android.com/reference/kotlin/androidx/work/WorkManager` time limit (currently ten minutes). After `provideContent` is called, the composition continues to run and recompose for about 45 seconds. When UI interactions or update requests are received, additional time is added to process these requests.

If this function is called concurrently with itself, the previous call will throw `https://kotlinlang.org/api/kotlinx.coroutines/kotlinx-coroutines-core/kotlinx.coroutines/-cancellation-exception/index.html` and the new content will replace it. This function should only be called from `https://developer.android.com/reference/kotlin/androidx/glance/appwidget/GlanceAppWidget#provideGlance(android.content.Context,androidx.glance.GlanceId)`.

private fun loadCachedStatus(context: Context): UserStatus {
    val prefs = context.getSharedPreferences("widget_cache", Context.MODE_PRIVATE)
    val feeling = prefs.getString("feeling", "")
    val hasData = !feeling.isNullOrEmpty()
    return UserStatus(
        doing = if (hasData) prefs.getString("doing", "") ?: "" else "",
        customDoing = if (hasData) prefs.getString("customDoing", "") ?: "" else "",
        feeling = if (hasData) feeling else "",
        feelingColor = if (hasData) prefs.getString("feelingColor", "#FFD93D") ?: "#FFD93D" else "#FFD93D",
        feelingEmoji = if (hasData) prefs.getString("feelingEmoji", "😊") ?: "😊" else "",
        note = if (hasData) prefs.getString("note", "") ?: "" else "",
        updatedAt = prefs.getLong("updatedAt", System.currentTimeMillis())
    )
}
```

class MyAppWidget : GlanceAppWidget() {

    override suspend fun provideGlance(context: Context, id: GlanceId) {

        // Use `withContext` to switch to another thread for long running
        // operations.

        provideContent {
            // create your AppWidget here
            Text("Hello World")
        }
    }
}

```

}

```

Now, let me think about this more carefully. The key issue might be:

In Glance 1.1.x with WorkManager-based sessions:
- When `updateAll(context)` is called, it checks if there's already a running WorkManager worker for the widget
- If a worker IS running, `updateAll` is a no-op (it doesn't restart `provideGlance`)
- If no worker is running, it enqueues a new one

The problem could be: when the widget is FIRST placed on the home screen, the system calls `onUpdate` which starts a WorkManager worker. This worker calls `provideGlance`, which reads SharedPreferences and renders the widget. If the SharedPreferences are empty (no pairing yet), the widget renders the default state. That's fine.

But when the user pairs and `updateWidgetCache` is called:
1. It writes to SharedPreferences
2. It calls `StandByWidget().updateAll(context)`

At this point, the WorkManager worker from step 1 might still be running (the 45-second composition window). In Glance 1.1.x, `updateAll` does NOT restart the running worker. So the widget continues to show the OLD content (empty state).

BUT - the user says the widget shows "can't load widget", which is different from "showing old content".

"Can't load widget" means the widget FAILED to render entirely. This could be because:
1. The WorkManager worker crashed
2. The composition threw an exception

This means calling `provideContent` concurrently throws `CancellationException`. If there's an old Glance session still running and a new `provideGlance` is called, the old `provideContent` might already have been cancelled. But this shouldn't cause "can't load widget" - it would just use the new content.

1. **Exception in `provideGlance`** → Check for potential runtime issues

修复完成。以下是改动：

## 修复内容

### 1. `standby_widget_info.xml` — 添加 `android:initialLayout`
```xml
android:initialLayout="@layout/glance_default_loading_layout"
```
使用 Glance 库自带的加载布局，防止系统在 WorkManager session 启动前直接显示 "can't load widget"。

### 2. `StandByWidget.kt` — 加 try/catch + Log 日志
- `provideGlance` 最外层包了 `try/catch`，异常时不再崩溃，而是渲染一个降级 fallback UI（💕 StandBy Us）
- 关键节点都加了 `Log.d/e`，下次遇到问题可以直接在 Logcat 过滤 `StandByWidget` 看哪里挂的
- `loadCachedStatus` 也加了 try/catch，SharedPreferences 读取出异常时返回空 `UserStatus()`

### 3. `StatusRepository.kt` — 写缓存 + 刷新 widget 原子化
- `updateWidgetCache` 改成 `suspend fun`，保证 `commit()`（同步写 SharedPreferences）完成后才调用 `updateAll()`
- 去掉了无管理的 `CoroutineScope(Dispatchers.IO).launch`泄漏
- 加了 Log 日志，可追踪 widget 缓存写入和刷新触发

**BUILD SUCCESSFUL** — 无报错。现在重新部署到设备上，Logcat 过滤 `StandByWidget` 和 `StandByStatusRepo` 就能看到 widget 的实际运行情况。

4. Widget content is updated

Looking at the Android source code for `AppWidgetHostView.updateAppWidget()`:
```java
public void updateAppWidget(RemoteViews remoteViews) {
    applyRemoteViews(remoteViews, true);
}
```

And `applyRemoteViews` calls `getDefaultView()` if `remoteViews` is null. Then it tries to update.

But the key is: the `APPWIDGET_UPDATE` broadcast is ALWAYS sent when a widget is added, regardless of whether `getDefaultView()` succeeds.

However, `getDefaultView()` failing might cause the launcher to not properly set up the widget host view, which could prevent subsequent updates from being applied.

Actually, looking at the Android Launcher3 source:
```java
protected View getDefaultView() {
    ...
    try {
        if (mInfo.initialLayout != 0) {
            return mLayoutInflater.inflate(mInfo.initialLayout, this, false);
        }
    } catch (Exception e) {
        Log.w(TAG, "Error inflating AppWidget " + mInfo, e);
    }
    return null;
}
```

If `getDefaultView()` returns null (because initialLayout is 0 or inflate fails), the launcher calls `onDefaultViewCreated(null)`. Then the widget waits for the actual RemoteViews update.

So the issue is NOT with the initial layout. The widget should still receive updates even if the initial layout fails.

In the previous version, `updateWidgetCache` used:
```kotlin
CoroutineScope(Dispatchers.IO).launch {
    StandByWidget().updateAll(context)
}
```

This launches a coroutine on IO dispatcher. `updateAll` is a suspend function. If the coroutine throws, the exception is silently caught by the CoroutineScope (since there's no exception handler). So if `updateAll` fails, we'd never know.

In my fix, I changed it to:
```kotlin
StandByWidget().updateAll(context)
```

As a suspend function inside `updateWidgetCache` (now also suspend). If it throws, it propagates to `fetchFromRemote()` which catches it. So any `updateAll` failure would now be caught.

But this only helps if `updateWidgetCache` is being called, which only happens when partner status data arrives.

Let me also add logging to the `StandByWidgetReceiver` to confirm whether `onUpdate` is being called. Actually, `GlanceAppWidgetReceiver` handles `onUpdate` automatically. I could add logging by overriding `onUpdate` in our receiver.

Actually, I think the most impactful fix right now is:
1. Add logging to the receiver's lifecycle

所有修改完成，BUILD SUCCESSFUL。总结：

## 修改文件一览

| 文件 | 改动 |
|------|------|
| `res/xml/standby_widget_info.xml` | 加了 `android:initialLayout="@layout/widget_loading"` |
| `res/layout/widget_loading.xml` | **新建** — 自定义 loading 布局（居中 ProgressBar + 暖色背景） |
| `StandByWidget.kt` | 加了 `try/catch` + `Log` 到 `provideGlance` 和 `loadCachedStatus`，异常时降级渲染默认 UI |
| `StandByWidgetReceiver.kt` | 加了 `Log` 到 `onUpdate`/`onEnabled`/`onDisabled` |
| `StatusRepository.kt` | `updateWidgetCache` 改为 `suspend`，用 `commit()` 替代 `apply()`，移除协程泄漏 |
| `StandByApplication.kt` | `onCreate()` 中主动触发 `StandByWidget().updateAll(this)` |

## 关键诊断

**Logcat 核心错误**:
```
Resources$NotFoundException: Resource ID #0x0
```
→ Glance 库的 `@layout/glance_default_loading_layout` 在小部件 metadata 上下文中无法解析（ID=0），系统直接报错 → **已用自定义 layout 替代**。

**日志中没有 `StandByWidget` 字样** → `provideGlance` 从未被调用 → Glance WorkManager-based session 没起来 → **已在 Application.onCreate() 中主动触发 `updateAll()`**。

部署后关注 Logcat 过滤 `StandByWidget`、`StandByWidgetReceiver`、`StandByApp`，应该能看到完整的 widget 生命周期日志了。

[Project README: E:\AndroidProject\StandByUs\README.md]
# StandBy Us

**情侣状态分享 App** — 两人之间的实时状态共享，支持心情、活动记录和桌面小组件。

---

## 项目简介

StandBy Us 是一个 Android 私密状态共享应用，专为两人使用设计。你可以实时分享当前的心情和正在做的事，对方的更新也会即时呈现在你的首页上。

- 灵感来源：[StandBy](https://standby.vip/#/PC)
- 技术栈：Kotlin + Jetpack Compose + Supabase

---

## 技术栈

| 层级     | 技术                            | 用途        |
| ------ | ----------------------------- | --------- |
| UI     | Jetpack Compose + Material3   | 声明式 UI    |
| 架构     | MVVM (ViewModel + Repository) | 业务逻辑分层    |
| DI     | Hilt                          | 依赖注入      |
| 本地存储   | Room                          | 状态缓存      |
| 后端     | Supabase REST API             | 数据同步      |
| 网络     | OkHttp                        | HTTP 客户端  |
| 小组件    | Glance                        | 桌面 Widget |
| 最低 SDK | Android 12 (API 31)           |           |
| 目标 SDK | Android 15 (API 35)           |           |

---

## 架构概览

```
app/
├── MainActivity.kt              # 入口 Activity + 庆祝动画集成

<type>file</type>
<content>
1: # Widget
2: 
3: Glance AppWidget showing partner's latest status with click-to-open.
4: 
5: ## STRUCTURE
6: 
7: ```
8: widget/
9: ├── StandByWidget.kt        # Glance composable widget
10: └── StandByWidgetReceiver.kt # BroadcastReceiver for updates
11: ```
12: 
13: ## NOTES
14: 
15: - Reads from `widget_cache` SharedPreferences
16: - Shows partner's feeling emoji + name + doing text
17: - Click to open MainActivity
18: - Auto-refreshes when partner status changes (6s polling)
19: - Shows placeholder text when no partner data yet
20: 
21: ## DATA FLOW
22: 
23: ```

Domain data classes and enums.

## STRUCTURE

```
model/
├── UserStatus.kt          # Core status data class
├── PairingInfo.kt         # Pair link data class
├── Feeling.kt             # 16 moods (displayName, emoji, colors)

└── AlbumPhoto.kt          # Photo metadata
```

| Model | Fields | Used by |
|-------|--------|---------|

| `Feeling` | HAPPY, SAD, TIRED, SICK, RELAXED, MISSING, SLEEPING, HUSTLING, THINKING, KISS, ANGRY, ANXIOUS, WATCHING, UPSET, LOVE, BORED | PostStatus, Home |
| `Doing` | WORKING, OVERTIME, STUDYING, SLEEPING, EATING, EXERCISING, COMMUTING, MOVIE, GAMING, DAYDREAMING, BOARD_GAME, CUSTOM | PostStatus |

79:     fun observeStatus(userId: String, updateWidget: Boolean = false): Flow<UserStatus?> = callbackFlow {
80:         Log.d(TAG, "observeStatus start for $userId updateWidget=$updateWidget")
81: 
82:         suspend fun fetchFromRemote() {
83:             try {
84:                 val results = supabaseService.query(
85:                     TABLE,
86:                     "userId=eq.$userId&order=updatedAt.desc&limit=1"
87:                 )
88:                 if (results.isNotEmpty()) {
89:                     val status = UserStatus.fromMap(results[0])
90:                     trySend(status)
91:                     statusDao.upsertStatus(status.toEntity())
92:                     if (updateWidget) {
93:                         updateWidgetCache(status)
94:                     }
95:                 } else {
96:                     Log.d(TAG, "observeStatus no remote data for $userId")
97:                     val cached = statusDao.getStatus(userId)?.toUserStatus()
98:                     if (cached != null) trySend(cached)
99:                 }
100:             } catch (e: Exception) {
101:                 Log.e(TAG, "observeStatus remote failed: ${e.message}")
102:                 val cached = statusDao.getStatus(userId)?.toUserStatus()
103:                 if (cached != null) trySend(cached)
104:             }
105:         }
106: 
107:         // 首次立即执行
108:         fetchFromRemote()
109: 
110:         // 然后定时轮询
111:         val job = CoroutineScope(Dispatchers.IO).launch {
112:             delay(POLL_MS) // 等一轮再查
113:             while (isActive) {
114:                 fetchFromRemote()
115:                 delay(POLL_MS)
116:             }
117:         }
118:         awaitClose { job.cancel() }
119:     }
120: }

135:     suspend fun query(table: String, query: String): List<Map<String, Any>> {

139:             .build()

143:     private suspend fun executeAndParse(request: Request): List<Map<String, Any>> = withContext(Dispatchers.IO) {
144:         val response = client.newCall(request).execute()
145:         val bodyStr = response.body?.string() ?: throw IOException("空响应")
146:         if (!response.isSuccessful) {
147:             throw IOException("Supabase 错误 (${response.code}): $bodyStr")
148:         }
149:         parseJsonArray(bodyStr)
150:     }
151: 
152:     private fun parseJsonArray(json: String): List<Map<String, Any>> {
153:         val arr = JSONArray(json)
154:         return (0 until arr.length()).map { i ->
155:             val obj = arr.getJSONObject(i)
156:             obj.keys().asSequence().associate { key ->
157:                 key to parseValue(obj.get(key))
158:             }
159:         }
160:     }
161: 
162:     private fun parseValue(value: Any): Any = when {
163:         value is Int || value is Long -> value
164:         value is Double -> {
165:             val l = value.toLong()
166:             if (l.toDouble() == value) l else value
167:         }
168:         value is JSONObject -> "{}"
169:         value is JSONArray -> "[]"
170:         else -> value
171:     }

## Complete Widget Architecture & "Can't Load Widget" Root Cause

### Data Flow (as designed)
```
partner status published → Supabase REST API
  → HomeViewModel collects partnerStatus (6s poll via callbackFlow)
    → StatusRepository.observeStatus(updateWidget=true)
      → updateWidgetCache() writes widget_cache SharedPreferences
        → StandByWidget().updateAll(context) triggers Glance recompose
          → StandByWidget.provideGlance() reads widget_cache, renders UI
```

### Critial Issue: Widget Cache is **only populated while HomeScreen is alive**

The `widget_cache` SharedPreferences is written exclusively in `StatusRepository.updateWidgetCache()` (line 57-72 of `StatusRepository.kt`). This is called from `observeStatus()` only when `updateWidget=true`, which is only triggered by `HomeViewModel.partnerStatus`. This flow is only collected while `HomeScreen` is active and the app is in the foreground.

**Three conditions must ALL be true for the widget to have data:**
1. The app must have been opened at least once
2. HomeScreen must currently be foreground-active (collecting the flow)
3. The user must be paired (partnerUserId must be non-empty)

### Why "Can't Load Widget" Occurs

**Primary cause**: The widget's `loadCachedStatus()` returns an *empty* `UserStatus` (all fields blank) when `widget_cache` was never populated. The widget then renders with `hasData=false`, showing placeholder text. This is NOT a crash — it's the expected empty state.

The actual "can't load widget" error (Android's default AppWidget error state) would appear if:

1. **An uncaught exception escapes `provideGlance()`** — possible paths:
   - `ColorProvider(android.graphics.Color.parseColor("#FFD93D"))` in the catch block — the fallback color is valid, so this won't throw.
   - Any Glance runtime exception during composition (e.g., missing resource, incompatible library version).

2. **The `widget_cache` read itself throws** — `getSharedPreferences` can theoretically throw on some OEM ROMs if storage is corrupted, but this is rare.

3. **Glance library initialization failure** — If the glance runtime fails to load (e.g., ProGuard stripping, library conflict), the widget would show "can't load widget".

**Most likely diagnosis**: If the widget shows "can't load widget" *immediately after adding it* (even before the app has been opened), this is normal — the cache is empty. If it shows the error *after the app has been used*, the issue is likely:

- **App was recently installed / Room DB was wiped** (via `fallbackToDestructiveMigration()`) and the widget cache was never populated
- **The user hasn't opened the app** since the last data sync — the 30-minute system auto-update interval re-renders the widget with whatever is in the stale (or empty) `widget_cache`
- **A Glance composable rendering error** — the `48.sp` fontSize or `ColorProvider` might be problematic in some Glance versions

### Secondary Issues Found

| Issue | File | Line |
|-------|------|------|
| `CoroutineScope(Dispatchers.IO).launch` in `updateWidgetCache()` creates unmanaged scope (no cancellation, memory leak) | `StatusRepository.kt` | 69 |
| Widget cache only written when HomeScreen is actively polling — no data if app was killed | `StatusRepository.kt` | 92-93 |
| `getDeviceId()` called in `StandByApplication.onCreate()` but not passed to DI system — SupabaseService has no ApplicationContext until explicitly passed | `SupabaseService.kt` | 73-83 |
| `supabaseService.getCachedDeviceId()` might return empty string if `getDeviceId(context)` was never called first | `SupabaseService.kt` | 86 |
| No `@AndroidEntryPoint` annotation on widget receiver (GlanceAppWidgetReceiver doesn't need one, but worth confirming) | `StandByWidgetReceiver.kt` | 6 |
| `glance-material3` dependency exists but is unused by the widget | `build.gradle.kts` | 61 |
</answer>

<next_steps>
**To diagnose the exact error:**
1. Run the debug build with the widget added — check `logcat` for `Exception` traces containing `provideGlance` or `StandByWidget`
2. Check if `widget_cache` SharedPreferences has any entries via `adb shell dumpsys dropbox | grep widget` or by adding a diagnostic read in `StandByWidgetReceiver.onUpdate()`
3. Verify the Glance library is not being stripped by ProGuard in release builds

**To fix the empty cache problem:**
1. Add a fallback: in `StandByWidget.loadCachedStatus()`, if `widget_cache` is empty, try reading from Room database directly (inject `StatusDao` or use `ContentProvider`)
2. Or: add a `WorkManager` periodic task that polls Supabase every 15-30 minutes and writes to `widget_cache`, independent of the HomeScreen lifecycle
3. Or: store the last known partner status in a way that survives app kills (Room already does this — the widget just needs to query Room instead of SharedPreferences)
</next_steps>
</results>

1. Extend the `AppWidget` receiver from `GlanceAppWidgetReceiver`:

class MyAppWidgetReceiver : GlanceAppWidgetReceiver() {
    override val glanceAppWidget: GlanceAppWidget = TODO("Create GlanceAppWidget")

1. Register the provider of the app widget in your `AndroidManifest.xml` file
and the associated metadata file:
[...]
The only difference for Glance is that there is no `initialLayout` XML, but
you must define one. You can use the predefined loading layout provided in
[...]
the library:
[...]
## Define `GlanceAppWidget`
[...]
1. Create a new class that extends from `GlanceAppWidget` and overrides the
`provideGlance` method. This is the method where you can load data that is
needed to render your widget:
[...]
> [!NOTE]
> Note: `provideGlance` runs on the main thread. To perform any long running operations in `provideGlance`, switch to another thread using `withContext`. See Use coroutines for main-safety for more details on how to run outside of the main thread.
[...]
```kotlin
class MyAppWidget : GlanceAppWidget() {

    override suspend fun provideGlance(context: Context, id: GlanceId) {

        // In this method, load data needed to render the AppWidget.
        // Use `withContext` to switch to another thread for long running
        // operations.

        provideContent {
            // create your AppWidget here
            Text("Hello World")
        }
    }
}

```
[...]
1. Instantiate it in the `glanceAppWidget` on your `GlanceAppWidgetReceiver`:

```kotlin
class MyAppWidgetReceiver : GlanceAppWidgetReceiver() {

    // Let MyAppWidgetReceiver know which GlanceAppWidget to use
    override val glanceAppWidget: GlanceAppWidget = MyAppWidget()
}

```
[...]
You've now configured an `AppWidget` using Glance.
[...]
class MyAppWidget : GlanceAppWidget() {

    override suspend fun provideGlance(context: Context, id: GlanceId) {
        // Load data needed to render the AppWidget.
        // Use `withContext` to switch to another thread for long running
        // operations.

        provideContent {
            // create your AppWidget here
            MyContent()
        }
    }

    @Composable
    private fun MyContent() {
        Column(
            modifier = GlanceModifier.fillMaxSize(),
            verticalAlignment = Alignment.Top,
            horizontalAlignment = Alignment.CenterHorizontally
        ) {
            Text(text = "Where to?", modifier = GlanceModifier.padding(12.dp))
            Row(horizontalAlignment = Alignment.CenterHorizontally) {
                Button(
                    text = "Home",
                    onClick = actionStartActivity<MyActivity>()
                )
                Button(
                    text = "Work",
                    onClick = actionStartActivity<MyActivity>()
                )
            }
        }
    }
}

}

### provideGlance
[...]
```
abstract suspend fun provideGlance(context: Context, id: GlanceId): Unit
```
[...]
Override this function to provide the Glance Composable.
[...]
This is a good place to load any data needed to render the Composable. Use provideContent to provide the Composable once the data is ready.
[...]
provideGlance is run in the background as a androidx.work.CoroutineWorker in response to calls to update and updateAll, as well as requests from the Launcher. Before`provideContent` is called,`provideGlance` is subject to the typical androidx.work.WorkManager time limit (currently ten minutes). After`provideContent` is called, the composition continues to run and recompose for about 45 seconds. When UI interactions or update requests are received, additional time is added to process these requests.
[...]
Note: update and updateAll do not restart`provideGlance` if it is already running. As a result, you should load initial data before calling`provideContent`, and then observe your sources of data within the composition (e.g. androidx.compose.runtime.collectAsState). This ensures that your widget will continue to update while the composition is active. When you update your data source from elsewhere in the app, make sure to call`update` in case a Worker for this widget is not currently running.
[...]
### providePreview
[...]
```
open
[...]
suspend fun providePreview(context: Context, widgetCategory: Int): Unit
[...]
Override this function to provide a Glance Composable that will be used when running this widget in preview mode. Use provideContent to provide the composable once the data is ready.
[...]
In order to generate and publish the previews for a provider, use setWidgetPreviews. You can use composeForPreview to generate a RemoteViews from this Composable without publishing it.
[...]
The given widgetCategory value will be one of AppWidgetProviderInfo.WIDGET_CATEGORY_HOME_SCREEN, AppWidgetProviderInfo.WIDGET_CATEGORY_KEYGUARD, or AppWidgetProviderInfo.WIDGET_CATEGORY_SEARCHBOX, or some combination of all three. This indicates what kind of widget host this preview can be used for. widgetCategory corresponds to the categories passed to setWidgetPreviews.
[...]
```
suspend fun update(context: Context, id: GlanceId): Unit
[...]
Run the composition in provideGlance and send the result to AppWidgetManager.

### GlanceAppWidget

Runs the composition in GlanceAppWidget.providePreview one time and translate it to a RemoteViews. This function can be used to test the preview layout of a GlanceAppWidget.

List<DpSize
[...]
, Unmanaged
[...]
<RemoteViews

Returns a Flow that, on collection, starts a composition session for this GlanceAppWidget and emits RemoteViews for each result. The composition is closed when the flow is cancelled.
[...]
### GlanceAppWidget.provideContent
[...]
```
suspend fun GlanceAppWidget.provideContent(    content: @Composable @GlanceComposable () -> Unit): Nothing
```
[...]
Provides content to the Glance host, suspending until the Glance session is shut down.
[...]
If this function is called concurrently with itself, the previous call will throw CancellationException and the new content will replace it. This function should only be called from GlanceAppWidget.provideGlance.

URL: https://android.googlesource.com/platform/frameworks/support/+/d81265bf9bec8d9283dd07b21686149676d9b57d/glance/glance-appwidget/src/main/java/androidx/glance/appwidget/GlanceAppWidget.kt
Published: N/A
Author: N/A
Highlights:
| * The UI is defined by calling [provideContent] from within [provideGlance]. When the widget is |
| * requested, the composition is run and translated into a [RemoteViews] which is then sent to the |
| * [AppWidgetManager]. |
| * |
[...]
| /** |
| * Override this function to provide the Glance Composable. |
| * |
| * This is a good place to load any data needed to render the Composable. Use [provideContent] |
| * to provide the Composable once the data is ready. |
| * |
| * [provideGlance] is run in the background as a [androidx.work.CoroutineWorker] in response to |
| * calls to [update] and [updateAll], as well as requests from the Launcher. Before |
| * `provideContent` is called, `provideGlance` is subject to the typical |
| * [androidx.work.WorkManager] time limit (currently ten minutes). After `provideContent` is |
| * called, the composition continues to run and recompose for about 45 seconds. When UI |
| * interactions or update requests are received, additional time is added to process these |
| * requests. |
| * |
| * Note: [update] and [updateAll] do not restart `provideGlance` if it is already running. As a |
| * result, you should load initial data before calling `provideContent`, and then observe your |
| * sources of data within the composition (e.g. [androidx.compose.runtime.collectAsState]). This |
| * ensures that your widget will continue to update while the composition is active. When you |
| * update your data source from elsewhere in the app, make sure to call `update` in case a |
| * Worker for this widget is not currently running. |
| * |
| * @sample androidx.glance.appwidget.samples.provideGlanceSample |
| * @sample androidx.glance.appwidget.samples.provideGlancePeriodicWorkSample |
| */ |
| abstract suspend fun provideGlance( |
| context: Context, |
| id: GlanceId, |
| ) |
[...]
| /** Defines the handling of sizes. */ |
| open val sizeMode: SizeMode = SizeMode.Single |
[...]
| /** Data store for widget data specific to the view. */ |
| open val stateDefinition:
[...]
StateDefinition<*>? = PreferencesGl
[...]
StateDefinition |
[...]
| /** Run the composition in [provideGlance] and send the result to [AppWidgetManager]. */ |
| suspend fun update(context: Context, id: GlanceId) { |
| require(id is AppWidgetId && id.isRealId) { "Invalid Glance ID" } |
| update(context, id.appWidgetId) |
| } |
[...]
| /** Internal version of [update], to be used by the broadcast receiver directly. */ |
[...]
| internal suspend fun update( |
| context: Context, |
| appWidgetId: Int, |
| options: Bundle? = null, |
| ) { |
| Tracing.beginGlanceAppWidgetUpdate() |
| val glanceId = AppWidgetId(appWidgetId) |
| sessionManager.runWithLock { |
| if (!isSessionRunning(context, glanceId.toSessionKey())) { |
| startSession(context, createAppWidgetSession(glanceId, options)) |
| return@runWithLock |
| } |
[...]
| val session = getSession(glanceId.toSessionKey()) as AppWidgetSession |
| session.updateGlance() |
| } |
| } |
[...]
| /** |
| * Provides [content] to the Glance host, suspending until the Glance session is shut down. |
| * |
| * If this function is called concurrently with itself, the previous call will throw |
| * [CancellationException] and the new content will replace it. This function should only be called |
| * from [GlanceAppWidget.provideGlance]. |
| * |
| * TODO: make this a protected member once b/206013293 is fixed. |
| */ |
| suspend fun GlanceAppWidget.provideContent( |
| content: @Composable @GlanceComposable () -> Unit |
| ): Nothing { |
| coroutineContext[ContentReceiver]?.provideContent(content) |
| ?: error( |
| "provideContent requires a ContentReceiver and should only be called from " + |
| "GlanceAppWidget.provideGlance" |
| ) |
| } |

Title: Manage and update GlanceAppWidget  |  Jetpack Compose  |  Android Developers
URL: https://developer.android.com/develop/ui/compose/glance/glance-app-widget
Published: N/A
Author: N/A
Highlights:
to update `GlanceAppWidget` and manage its
state.
[...]
The provided `GlanceAppWidget` class is instantiated whenever the widget is
created or requires an update, so it should be stateless and passive.
[...]
Whenever the state or the data changes, it is the app's responsibility to notify
and update the widget. See Update GlanceAppWidget for more information.
[...]
## Update `GlanceAppWidget`
[...]
You can request to update your widget content using `GlanceAppWidget`. As
explained in the Manage `GlanceAppWidget` state section, app
widgets are hosted in a different process. Glance translates the content into
actual `RemoteViews` and sends them to the host. To update the content, Glance
must recreate the `RemoteViews` and send them again.
[...]
To send the update, call the `update` method of the `GlanceAppWidget` instance,
providing the `context` and the `glanceId`:
[...]
```kotlin
MyAppWidget().update(context, glanceId)

```
[...]
To obtain the `glanceId`, query the `GlanceAppWidgetManager`:
[...]
```kotlin
val manager = GlanceAppWidgetManager(context)
val widget = GlanceSizeModeWidget()
val glanceIds = manager.getGlanceIds(widget.javaClass)
glanceIds.forEach { glanceId ->
    widget.update(context, glanceId)
}

```
[...]
Alternatively, use one of the `GlanceAppWidget update` extensions:
[...]
```kotlin
// Updates all placed instances of MyAppWidget
MyAppWidget().updateAll(context)

// Iterate over all placed instances of MyAppWidget and update if the state of
// the instance matches the given predicate
MyAppWidget().updateIf<State>(context) { state ->
    state == State.Completed
}

```
[...]
These methods can be called from any part of your application. Because they are
`suspend` functions, we recommend launching them outside of the main thread
scope. In the following example, they are launched in a `CoroutineWorker`:
[...]
```kotlin
class DataSyncWorker(
    val context: Context,
    val params: WorkerParameters,
) : CoroutineWorker(context, params) {

    override suspend fun doWork(): Result {
        // Fetch data or do some work and then update all instance of your widget
        MyAppWidget().updateAll(context)
        return Result.success()
    }
}
[...]
### When to update widgets
[...]
either immediately or periodically.
[...]
In these cases, call the `update` method as described in this guide.
[...]
Your widget can update periodically when your app isn't awake. For example:
[...]
- Use `updatePeriodMillis` to update the widget up to once every 30 minutes.
[...]
- Use `WorkManager` to schedule more frequent updates, such as every
[...]
15 minutes.
[...]
- Update the widget in response to a broadcast.
```

Adds similar variants for their`assertHasXXX` counterparts. (Ieca63)

[...]
### 版本 1.1.0-rc01
[...]
- 为 Glance 的 Preview 注释添加了宽度和高度参数。将 1.1.0 移至候选版本。
[...]
API 变更
[...]
- 重命名了`Viewfinder`的`ImplementationMode`枚举，以更好地反映底层实现，并为`TransformationInfo.sourceRotation`添加了固定常量 (Ic6149)
- 添加了用于为`FontStyle`设置自定义权重值的 API。(I7390a)
- 移除了对 Glance Wear 磁贴预览的支持。(I3850a)
- 为 Glance`@Preview`添加了宽度和高度参数。(Ibabe8)
- 向 Scaffold 添加了可选参数。(If753f)
[...]
bug 修复
[...]
- 修复了在向后兼容模式下导致`ViewGroups`出现渲染问题的 bug (I8de92)
[...]
- 实验性`SharedTransitionScope`现在是一个接口，而不是一个类。（ Iaf856、 b/338415048、 b/338414702）
[...]
alpha01
[...]
- 用于从合成中获取 RemoteViews 流的新 API，`runComposition`
[...]
- 新的修饰符和主题选项。
- 更高级别的组件，可实现更简单的布局。
- 不需要 UI Automator 的 Glance 单元测试库。可以直接测试 Glance 代码，而无需膨胀视图。
[...]
API 变更
[...]
- 添加了断言和过滤器，以实现对启动服务 / activity 或广播的点击操作的测试。还包括测试输入元素是否已勾选。(I3041c)
- 添加了`isLinearProgressIndicator`、`isIndeterminateLinearProgressIndicator`、`isIndeterminateCircularProgressIndicator`过滤条件以匹配进度指示器。添加了`hasAnyDescendants`过滤条件，用于测试节点在其子层次结构中是否具有与特定匹配器 (Ifd426) 匹配的后代
[...]
- 向 Glance 添加了按钮和图标按钮 API (I0fd6f)
- 添加了`onCompositionError`方法，开发者可以在发生错误时运行代码 (I9b56f)
- 移动未发布的 API。将修饰符从内部更改为公开但受库限制 (If2a08)
- 向测试`actionRunCallack`添加了`hasActionRunCallbackClickAction`过滤条件和`assertHasActionRunCallbackClickAction`断言。此外，还为与操作相关的测试过滤条件添加了其他简写变体函数 -`hasStartActivityClickAction (..)`、`hasStartServiceAction (..)`、`hasSendBroadcastAction (..)`。为其`assertHasXXX`对应项添加了类似的变体。(Ieca63)
[...]
- 添加了 scaffold 组件 (I8a736)
- 更新了`TitleBar`- 文本和图标可单独着色。(Ia0a60)
- 在语义中添加了`testTag`修饰符，以便在单元测试中使用。(I8f62f)
- 添加了`runGlanceAppWidgetUnitTest`，该`runGlanceAppWidgetUnitTest`提供在`GlanceAppWidgetUnitTest`上调用方法（例如`provideComposable`，用于提供一个用于测试的小型隔离可组合项；`onNode`，用于在提供的内容中查找 Glance 可组合元素）的范围。这样一来，您就可以为 appWidget 中的各个可组合函数编写单元测试，以验证在给定特定输入的情况下，该函数是否会输出预期的一组 Glance 可组合元素。(I2f682)
[...]
- 添加了用于为按钮着色的新 API。在 1.0 正式版发布之前，此功能应处于实验阶段。(I92523)
- 向`clickable`修饰符添加了替换项。(Iacecf)
- 添加了新的`TopBar`组件 (Ibd361)
- 添加了`GlanceAppWidget.runComposition`（ I6344c、 b/298066147）
- 向 Glance 主题添加了新的`widgetBackground`颜色角色。(Ia2ab8)
[...]
bug 修复
[...]
API 变更
[...]
bug 修复
[...]
API 变更

Title: [Android] Upgrade androidx.glance to 1.1.1 · Issue #337 - GitHub
URL: https://github.com/ABausG/home_widget/issues/337
Published: 2025-03-01T02:16:25.000Z
Author: gonzalogauto
Highlights:
# Issue: ABausG/home_widget #337

- Repository: ABausG/home_widget | Flutter Package for Easier Creation of Home Screen Widgets | 942 stars | Dart

## [Android] Upgrade androidx.glance to 1.1.1

- Author: @gonzalogauto
- State: closed (completed)
- Labels: enhancement
- Reactions: 👍 2
- Created: 2025-03-01T02:16:25Z
- Updated: 2025-11-09T11:46:39Z
- Closed: 2025-11-09T11:46:39Z
- Closed by: @ABausG

### Problem to solve

There is no real problem to solve here. This is a feature request.

### Proposal

Would be nice to use `'androidx.glance:glance-appwidget:1.1.1'`.

This new version of glance-appwidget introduces really cool components such as Scaffold or TopBar that makes easier to build simple layouts.

Even when we follow the current docs we can see a warning that suggests to upgrade to `1.1.1`

[Image: Image | https://github.com/user-attachments/assets/a4315fdb-c9c5-4756-8dbd-d941a1fde160]

### More information

_No response_

### Which Platform would be improved?

- Android

### Other

- [ ] Are you interested in working on a PR for this?

---

### Timeline

**gonzalogauto** added label `enhancement` · Mar 1, 2025 at 2:16am

**@ABausG** commented · Nov 9, 2025 at 11:46am

> This should be fixed as home_widget now depends on "1.+"

**ABausG** closed this · Nov 9, 2025 at 11:46am

Title: glance_widget_android changelog | Flutter package
URL: https://pub.dev/packages/glance_widget_android/changelog
Published: 2026-01-09T17:31:05.000Z
Author: N/A
Highlights:
# Changelog #
[...]
## 1.0.0 #
[...]
- Complete all platform method overrides (was 7/18, now 18/18)
- Method channel namespace changed from`com.example.glance_widget` to`dev.glance.widget`
- Added`dispose()` override for resource cleanup
- Updated dependency:`glance_widget_platform_interface: ^1.0.0`
[...]
0.3.0 #
[...]
## 0.2.1 #
[...]
- Updated dependency on glance_widget_platform_interface to ^0.4.0
- JSON Serialization - Fixed list widget item parsing using proper JSON instead of delimiter-based parsing
- Error Handling - Added`UpdateResult` sealed class for structured error reporting
- Backward Compatibility - Legacy delimiter parsing preserved for existing widget data
- Glance 1.1.1 Compatibility - Fixed ColorProvider API for Compose Color
- Compose BOM - Added Compose BOM 2024.12.01 dependency for runtime compatibility
- CircularProgressIndicator - Changed to percentage display (Glance only supports indeterminate mode)
- isWidgetPushSupported - Added method stub (returns false, iOS-only feature)
- compileSdk - Updated to API 36
- Updated dependency on glance_widget_platform_interface to ^0.3.0

This enables you to write unit tests for individual composable functions in your appWidget to verify that given certain inputs the function outputs the intended set of glance composable elements. (I2f682)

- Moved`GlanceAppWidget` to a`WorkManager` session based update mechanism. Users of Glance for`AppWidgets` should now override`GlanceAppWidget.provideGlance` instead of the old`Content` method. As this now happens in a worker, this is now a good place to load resources, database or network items without having to have a separate worker.

- Compose your UI with the initial set of Glance Composables: Box, Row, Column, Text, Button, LazyColumn, Image, Spacer.

Title: GlanceAppWidget  |  API reference  |  Android Developers
URL: https://developer.android.com/reference/kotlin/androidx/glance/appwidget/GlanceAppWidget
Published: N/A
Author: N/A
Highlights:
The UI is defined by calling `https://developer.android.com/reference/kotlin/androidx/glance/appwidget/package-summary#(androidx.glance.appwidget.GlanceAppWidget).provideContent(kotlin.Function0)` from within `https://developer.android.com/reference/kotlin/androidx/glance/appwidget/GlanceAppWidget#provideGlance(android.content.Context,androidx.glance.GlanceId)`. When the widget is requested, the composition is run and translated into a `https://developer.android.com/reference/android/widget/RemoteViews.html` which is then sent to the `https://developer.android.com/reference/android/appwidget/AppWidgetManager.html`.
[...]
| `suspend https://kotlinlang.org/api/core/kotlin-stdlib/kotlin/-nothing/index.html` | `https://developer.android.com/reference/kotlin/androidx/glance/appwidget/GlanceAppWidget.https://developer.android.com/reference/kotlin/androidx/glance/appwidget/GlanceAppWidget#(androidx.glance.appwidget.GlanceAppWidget).provideContent(kotlin.Function0)( content: @https://developer.android.com/reference/kotlin/androidx/compose/runtime/Composable @https://developer.android.com/reference/kotlin/androidx/glance/GlanceComposable () -> https://kotlinlang.org/api/core/kotlin-stdlib/kotlin/-unit/index.html )` Provides `https://developer.android.com/reference/kotlin/androidx/glance/appwidget/package-summary#(androidx.glance.appwidget.GlanceAppWidget).provideContent(kotlin.Function0)` to the Glance host, suspending until the Glance session is shut down. |
[...]
This is a good place to load any data needed to render the Composable. Use `https://developer.android.com/reference/kotlin/androidx/glance/appwidget/package-summary#(androidx.glance.appwidget.GlanceAppWidget).provideContent(kotlin.Function0)` to provide the Composable once the data is ready.

[...]
background as a `https://developer.android.com/reference/kotlin/androidx/work/CoroutineWorker` in response to calls to `https://developer.android.com/reference/kotlin/androidx/glance/appwidget/GlanceAppWidget#update(android.content.Context,androidx.glance.GlanceId)` and `https://developer.android.com/reference/kotlin/androidx/glance/appwidget/package-summary#(androidx.glance.appwidget.GlanceAppWidget).updateAll(android.content.Context)`, as well as requests from the Launcher. Before `provideContent` is called, `provideGlance` is subject to the typical `https://developer.android.com/reference/kotlin/androidx/work/WorkManager` time limit (currently ten minutes). After `provideContent` is called, the composition continues to run and recompose for about 45 seconds. When UI interactions or update requests are received, additional time is added to process these requests.
[...]

This function should only be called from `https://developer.android.com/reference/kotlin/androidx/glance/appwidget/GlanceAppWidget#provideGlance(android.content.Context,androidx.glance.GlanceId)`.

Title: From RemoteViews to RemoteCompose: What’s Changing Under Glance’s Hood | by Luca Fioravanti | Mar, 2026 | Medium
URL: https://medium.com/@fioravanti.luka/glance-remoteviews-and-remotecompose-what-actually-changed-in-android-16-4afc4b63b0ad
Published: 2026-03-25T11:37:02.000Z
Author: Luca Fioravanti
Highlights:
If you build Android widgets with Jetpack Glance, you write what looks like Compose code. It isn’t. Glance composables produce an`Emittable` tree that gets translated into`RemoteViews`— the same`TextView`-in-a-`LinearLayout` system that has powered widgets since the beginning. Glance makes it nicer to write. It doesn't change what gets rendered.
[...]
Android 16 ships a new API called`RemoteViews.DrawInstructions` and a binary rendering engine called RemoteCompose. The idea: instead of inflating XML views across process boundaries, you send a compact byte array and the system paints it directly. No`TextView`, no`LinearLayout`, no view inflation.
[...]
A quick refresher. When you write a Glance widget:
[...]
```
class WeatherWidget : GlanceAppWidget() {    override suspend fun provideGlance(context: Context, id: GlanceId) {        provideContent {            Column(                modifier = GlanceModifier.fillMaxSize().padding(16.dp),            ) {                Text(                    text = "22C - Sunny",                    style = TextStyle(fontSize = 24.sp, fontWeight = FontWeight.Bold),                )                Spacer(modifier = GlanceModifier.height(8.dp))                Button(                    text = "Refresh",                    onClick = actionRunCallback<RefreshAction>(),                )            }        }    }}
```
[...]
These aren’t Compose UI composables. They produce Glance’s own`Emittable` tree, which gets translated to`RemoteViews`. Every`Text()` becomes a`TextView`, every`Row()` a`LinearLayout`. The constraints of`RemoteViews` apply: limited view types, max 10 children per container, no custom drawing, no canvas operations. Glance hides the ugliness but can't escape the boundaries.
[...]
Android 16 adds`RemoteViews.DrawInstructions`— a new way to populate a`RemoteViews` object. Instead of describing a tree of views, you pass a byte array containing a binary document. The platform includes a renderer at`com.android.internal.widget.remotecompose` that reads these bytes and paints the widget directly.
[...]
The Glance source tree on`androidx-main` already contains a full RemoteCompose backend — a`Backend` enum, a`GlanceRemoteComposeTranslator`, and a dual-path architecture that will pick RemoteCompose on API 36+ and fall back to RemoteViews on older devices:
[...]
```
// Backend.kt (in Glance source tree)public enum class Backend {    RemoteView,      // Traditional (API 21+)    RemoteCompose,   // Binary protocol (API 36+)}
```
[...]
When this ships, the translation pipeline changes from:
[...]
```
Composable -> Emittable tree -> RemoteViews (XML views)
```
[...]
```
Composable -> Emittable tree -> binary byte[] -> DrawInstructions -> RemoteViews
```
[...]
The output is still a`RemoteViews` object, so the`AppWidgetManager` API doesn't change. But the payload is different. Here's the code from the source tree that wraps the bytes:
[...]
```
// DrawInstructionRemoteViews.ktval drawInstructions = RemoteViews.DrawInstructions    .Builder(listOf(bytes))    .build()val remoteViews = RemoteViews(drawInstructions)
```
[...]
```
translation.actionMap.forEach { (actionId, pendingIntent) ->    remoteViews.setOnClickPendingIntent(actionId, pendingIntent)}
```
[...]
## This isn’t shipped yet
[...]
The key word above is “source tree.” None of this is in a published Glance release.
[...]
The latest Glance artifact (`glance-appwidget-1.2.0-rc01`, December 2025) contains zero RemoteCompose classes. No`Backend` enum, no translator, no dependency on`androidx.compose.remote`. A Glance widget running on an Android 16 emulator today still renders as`LinearLayout`+`TextView`— classic RemoteViews.
[...]
published and functional
[...]
0.0-
[...]
living inside the Android framework. The AndroidX version (`androidx.compose.remote`) is more ambitious. It has 155+ operations, additional layout types (flow layouts, collapsible containers, image layouts), and features the platform doesn't support yet

---

Title: Create a widget with Glance  |  Android Developers
URL: https://developer.android.com/codelabs/glance
Published: N/A
Author: N/A
Highlights:
Android uses a broadcast receiver to alert SociaLite that a widget was added, needs to be updated, or was removed. Glance provides an abstract receiver class, GlanceAppWidgetReceiver, which extends AppWidgetProvider.
[...]
`GlanceAppWidgetReceiver` implementations are also responsible for providing instances of`GlanceAppWidget`. This class renders Glance's composables into remote views.
[...]
1. Navigate to the`widget` package in`app/src/main/java/com/google/android/samples/socialite/`.
2. Open the`SociaLiteAppWidget` class. This class overrides the`provideGlance` method.
3. Replace the`TODO` with a call to`provideContent` and then pass your widget's composable function as a parameter. For now, the widget only displays the message`Hello World`, but you add more functionality later in this codelab.
[...]
```
package com.google.android.
[...]
import androidx.gl
[...]
.GlanceId
[...]
import androidx.glance.GlanceTheme
[...]
import androidx.glance.appwidget.GlanceAppWidget
[...]
import androidx.glance.appwidget.provideContent
[...]
class SociaLiteAppWidget : GlanceAppWidget() {
   override suspend fun provideGlance(context: Context, id: GlanceId) {
       provideContent {
           GlanceTheme {
               Text("Hello World")
           }
       }
   }
}
[...]
1. Open the`SociaLiteAppWidgetReceiver` class in the`widget` package. For now, your receiver provides an instance of your`SociaLiteWidget`, but you add more functionality in an upcoming section.
2. Replace the`TODO` with the`SociaLiteAppWidget()` constructor:
[...]
.appwidget.GlanceAppWidget
[...]
import androidx.glance.appwidget.GlanceAppWidgetReceiver
[...]
class SociaLiteAppWidgetReceiver : GlanceAppWidgetReceiver() {
   override val glanceAppWidget: GlanceAppWidget = SociaLiteAppWidget()
}
[...]
class SociaLiteAppWidget : GlanceAppWidget() {
   override suspend fun provideGlance(context: Context, id: GlanceId) {
       provideContent {
           GlanceTheme() {
               Content()
           }
       }
   }

   @Composable
   private fun Content() {
       Scaffold(titleBar = {TitleBar(startIcon = ImageProvider(R.drawable.ic_launcher_monochrome), title = "SociaLite")},
           modifier = GlanceModifier.fillMaxSize()) {
           Text("Hello World")
       }
   }
}
[...]
but in the next part you create a better
[...]
1. In the`provideGlance` method, outside of your composable, get a reference to the`WidgetModelRepository` and the current widget ID. Add the following lines before`provideContent` in the`SociaLiteAppWidget``provideGlance` method.
[...]
```
override suspend fun provideGlance(context: Context, id: GlanceId) {
   val widgetId = GlanceAppWidgetManager(context).getAppWidgetId(id)
   val repository = WidgetModelRepository.get(context)
[...]
1. In the`Content` composable function, add the repository and widget ID as parameters, and use them to load your model. Update the`Content` composable's function signature and add the following line:
[...]
```
private fun Content(repository: WidgetModelRepository, widgetId: Int) {
   val model = repository.loadModel(widgetId).collectAsState(Loading).value
[...]
You also need to update`provideGlance` to pass the widget ID and repository to`Content`.
[...]
Replace`provideGlance` with the following :
[...]
```
override suspend fun provideGlance(context: Context, id: GlanceId) {
        val widgetId = GlanceAppWidgetManager(context).getAppWidgetId(id)
        val repository = WidgetModelRepository.get(context)

        provideContent {
            GlanceTheme {
                Content(repository, widgetId)
            }
        }
    }
[...]
1. In your`Content` composable function, determine which state to show based on whether a model exists. Move the`Scaffold` and widget content into the`ZeroState` composable by replacing the`Scaffold` component and its content with this when block:
[...]
```
   when (model) {
       is WidgetModel -> {Text("Hello World")}
       else -> ZeroState(widgetId)
   }

Title: Create an app widget with Glance | Jetpack Compose
URL: https://developer.android.com/develop/ui/compose/glance/create-app-widget
Published: N/A
Author: N/A
Highlights:
> [!IMPORTANT]
> Key Point: Glance provides a modern approach to build app widgets using Compose, but is restricted by the limitations of `AppWidgets` and `RemoteViews`. Therefore, Glance uses different composables from the Jetpack Compose UI.
[...]
The only difference for Glance is that there is no `initialLayout` XML, but
you must define one. You can use the predefined loading layout provided in
the library:
[...]
## Define `GlanceAppWidget`
[...]
1. Create a new class that extends from `GlanceAppWidget` and overrides the
`provideGlance` method. This is the method where you can load data that is
needed to render your widget:
[...]
> [!NOTE]
> Note: `provideGlance` runs on the main thread. To perform any long running operations in `provideGlance`, switch to another thread using `withContext`. See Use coroutines for main-safety for more details on how to run outside of the main thread.
[...]
```kotlin
class MyAppWidget : GlanceAppWidget() {

    override suspend fun provideGlance(context: Context, id: GlanceId) {

        // In this method, load data needed to render the AppWidget.
        // Use `withContext` to switch to another thread for long running
        // operations.

        provideContent {
            // create your AppWidget here
            Text("Hello World")
        }
    }
}

```
[...]
1. Instantiate it in the `glanceAppWidget` on your `GlanceAppWidgetReceiver`:
[...]
```kotlin
class MyAppWidgetReceiver : GlanceAppWidgetReceiver() {

    // Let MyAppWidgetReceiver know which GlanceAppWidget to use
    override val glanceAppWidget: GlanceAppWidget = MyAppWidget()
}

```
[...]
You've now configured an `AppWidget` using Glance.
[...]
## Create UI
[...]
The following snippet demonstrates how to create the UI:
[...]
```kotlin
/* Import Glance Composables
 In the event there is a name clash with the Compose classes of the same name,
 you may rename the imports per https://kotlinlang.org/docs/packages.html#imports
 using the `as` keyword.

import androidx.glance.Button
import androidx.glance.layout.Column
import androidx.glance.layout.Row
import androidx.glance.text.Text
*/
class MyAppWidget : GlanceAppWidget() {

    override suspend fun provideGlance(context: Context, id: GlanceId) {
        // Load data needed to render the AppWidget.
        // Use `withContext` to switch to another thread for long running
        // operations.

        provideContent {
            // create your AppWidget here
            MyContent()
        }
    }

    @Composable
    private fun MyContent() {
        Column(
            modifier = GlanceModifier.fillMaxSize(),
            verticalAlignment = Alignment.Top,
            horizontalAlignment = Alignment.CenterHorizontally
        ) {
            Text(text = "Where to?", modifier = GlanceModifier.padding(12.dp))
            Row(horizontalAlignment = Alignment.CenterHorizontally) {
                Button(
                    text = "Home",
                    onClick = actionStartActivity<MyActivity>()
                )
                Button(
                    text = "Work",
                    onClick = actionStartActivity<MyActivity>()
                )
            }
        }
    }
}

```
[...]
The preceding code sample does the following:
[...]
- In the top level `Column`, items are placed vertically one after each other.
[...]
- The `Column` expands its size to match the available space (via the `GlanceModifier` and aligns its content to the top (`verticalAlignment`) and centers it horizontally (`horizontalAlignment`).
[...]
- The `Column`'s content is defined using the lambda. The order matters.
[...]
`Text` component with `
[...]
.dp` of padding.
[...]
look like:

 Figure
[...]
You can change the alignment values or apply different modifier values (such as
padding) to change the placement and size of the components. See the reference
documentation for a full list of components, parameters, and available
modifiers for each class.

Title: provideContent - Function in Glance App Widget
URL: https://composables.com/docs/androidx.glance/glance-appwidget/functions/provideContent
Published: N/A
Author: N/A
Highlights:
```kotlin
public suspend fun GlanceAppWidget.provideContent(
    content: @Composable @GlanceComposable () -> Unit
): Nothing

```
[...]
Provides `content` to the Glance host, suspending until the Glance session is shut down.
[...]
If this function is called concurrently with itself, the previous call will throw
`CancellationException` and the new content will replace it. This function should only be called
from GlanceAppWidget.provideGlance.
[...]
TODO: make this a protected member once b/206013293 is fixed.

Title: Manage and update GlanceAppWidget | Jetpack Compose
URL: https://developer.android.com/develop/ui/compose/glance/glance-app-widget
Published: N/A
Author: N/A
Highlights:
The following sections describe how to update `GlanceAppWidget` and manage its
state.
[...]
## Manage `GlanceAppWidget` state
[...]
The provided `GlanceAppWidget` class is instantiated whenever the widget is
created or requires an update, so it should be stateless and passive.
[...]
App widgets should be passive. Each application is responsible for managing the
data layer and handling the states, such as idle, loading, and error reflecting
in the widget UI.
[...]
```kotlin
class DestinationAppWidget : GlanceAppWidget() {

    // ...

    @Composable
    fun MyContent() {
        val repository = remember { DestinationsRepository.getInstance() }
        // Retrieve the cache data everytime the content is refreshed
        val destinations by repository.destinations.collectAsState(State.Loading)

        when (destinations) {
            is State.Loading -> {
                // show loading content
            }

            is State.Error -> {
                // show widget error content
            }

            is State.Completed -> {
                // show the list of destinations
            }
        }
    }
}
[...]
Whenever the state or the data changes, it is the app's responsibility to notify
and update the widget. See Update GlanceAppWidget for more information.
[...]
> [!NOTE]
> Note: See the Optimizations for updating widget content section in the app widgets guide to understand how and when to update.
[...]
## Update `GlanceAppWidget`
[...]
You can request to update your widget content using `GlanceAppWidget`. As
explained in the Manage `GlanceAppWidget` state section, app
widgets are hosted in a different process. Glance translates the content into
actual `RemoteViews` and sends them to the host. To update the content, Glance
must recreate the `RemoteViews` and send them again.
[...]
To send the update, call the `update` method of the `GlanceAppWidget` instance,
providing the `context` and the `glanceId`:
[...]
```kotlin
MyAppWidget().update(context, glanceId)
[...]
To obtain the `glanceId`, query the `GlanceAppWidgetManager`:
[...]
```kotlin
val manager = GlanceAppWidgetManager(context)
val widget = GlanceSizeModeWidget()
val glanceIds = manager.getGlanceIds(widget.javaClass)
glanceIds.forEach { glanceId ->
    widget.update(context, glanceId)
}
[...]
Alternatively, use one of the `GlanceAppWidget update` extensions:
[...]
```kotlin
// Updates all placed instances of MyAppWidget
MyAppWidget().updateAll(context)
[...]
// Iterate over all placed instances of MyAppWidget and update if the state of
// the instance matches the given predicate
MyAppWidget().updateIf<State>(context) { state ->
    state == State.Completed
}

```
[...]
These methods can be called from any part of your application. Because they are
`suspend` functions, we recommend launching them outside of the main thread
scope. In the following example, they are launched in a `CoroutineWorker`:
[...]
val context:
[...]
) : CoroutineWorker(
[...]
override suspend
[...]
Fetch data or do
[...]
then update all instance of your widget
[...]
Widget().updateAll(context
[...]
return
[...]
.success()
    }
}
[...]
### When to update widgets
[...]
Update widgets either immediately or periodically.
[...]
Your widget can update immediately when your app is awake. For example:
[...]
- When a user interacts with a widget, triggering an action, a lambda call, or an intent to launch an activity.
- When your user interacts with your app in the foreground, or while the app is already updating in response to a Firebase Cloud Messaging (FCM) message or a broadcast.
[...]
In these cases, call the `update` method as described in this guide.
[...]
Your widget can update periodically when your app isn't awake. For example:
[...]
- Use `updatePeriodMillis` to update the widget up to once every 30 minutes.
- Use `WorkManager` to schedule more frequent updates, such as every 15 minutes.
- Update the widget in response to a broadcast.

Highlights:
* |
| * The UI is defined by calling [provideContent] from within [provideGlance]. When the widget is |
| * requested, the composition is run and translated into a [RemoteViews] which is then sent to the |
| * [AppWidgetManager]. |
| * |
[...]
| /** |
| * Override this function to provide the Glance Composable. |
| * |
| * This is a good place to load any data needed to render the Composable. Use [provideContent] |
| * to provide the Composable once the data is ready. |
| * |
| * [provideGlance] is run in the background as a [androidx.work.CoroutineWorker] in response to |
| * calls to [update] and [updateAll], as well as requests from the Launcher. Before |
| * `provideContent` is called, `provideGlance` is subject to the typical |
| * [androidx.work.WorkManager] time limit (currently ten minutes). After `provideContent` is |
| * called, the composition continues to run and recompose for about 45 seconds. When UI |
| * interactions or update requests are received, additional time is added to process these |
| * requests. |
| * |
| * Note: [update] and [updateAll] do not restart `provideGlance` if it is already running. As a |
| * result, you should load initial data before calling `provideContent`, and then observe your |
| * sources of data within the composition (e.g. [androidx.compose.runtime.collectAsState]). This |
| * ensures that your widget will continue to update while the composition is active. When you |
| * update your data source from elsewhere in the app, make sure to call `update` in case a |
| * Worker for this widget is not currently running. |
| * |
| * @sample androidx.glance.appwidget.samples.provideGlanceSample |

Title: Jetpack Glance  |  Jetpack Compose  |  Android Developers
URL: https://developer.android.com/develop/ui/compose/glance
Published: N/A
Author: N/A
Highlights:
Jetpack Glance is a framework built on top of the Jetpack Compose runtime
that lets you develop and design app widgets using Kotlin APIs. App widgets
are miniature application views that can be embedded in other applications and
receive periodic updates.
 Figure 1. An information widget from a weather app.
[...]
Glance provides a set of composables to help you build responsive widgets for
the home screen quickly and with less code. The pages in this doc set describe
how to use Glance to build app widgets.
[...]
For more information on canonical layouts visit Canonical widget layouts.
For more information on making your widget high quality and discoverable,
see Widget quality.
[...]
| Caution: Glance requires Compose to be enabled and depends on Runtime, Graphics, and Unit UI Compose layers, but it's not directly interoperable with other existing Jetpack Compose UI elements. Avoid mixing the two.

---

- "Updated `GlanceAppWidget` to use `provideGlance` as the main entrypoint. `GlanceAppWidget.Content` is now deprecated."

From later in the release cycle:
- "Remove deprecated `GlanceAppWidget.Content` function" - This was the OLD `Content` method (a `@Composable` function inside `GlanceAppWidget`).

2. **The current official documentation** (create-app-widget guide and API reference) shows exactly the pattern the user is using:
```kotlin

        provideContent {
            // composable content
            Text("Hello World")
        }
    }
}
```

3. **The API reference for `GlanceAppWidget`** confirms:
- `provideGlance` is the abstract method to override (added in 1.0.0)
- `provideContent` is called from within `provideGlance`
- The old `GlanceAppWidget.Content` composable was deprecated in 1.1.0-alpha01 and then **removed**

So the user's pattern is actually **the CORRECT current pattern for Glance 1.1.1**. The evolution was:
- **Glance 1.0.x**: Had both `provideGlance` AND `GlanceAppWidget.Content` (`@Composable` function)
- **Glance 1.1.0-alpha01**: `Content` was deprecated, `provideGlance` became the main entrypoint
- **Glance 1.1.0 stable**: The old `Content` function was **removed entirely**
- **Glance 1.1.1**: Same API as 1.1.0, just protobuf security fix

So the user's pattern `provideGlance` + `provideContent` is exactly what Glance 1.1.1 expects. The old pattern with a `Content` composable inside `GlanceAppWidget` is gone.

[Project README: E:\AndroidProject\StandByUs\README.md]
# StandBy Us

**情侣状态分享 App** — 两人之间的实时状态共享，支持心情、活动记录和桌面小组件。

---

## 项目简介

StandBy Us 是一个 Android 私密状态共享应用，专为两人使用设计。你可以实时分享当前的心情和正在做的事，对方的更新也会即时呈现在你的首页上。

- 灵感来源：[StandBy](https://standby.vip/#/PC)
- 技术栈：Kotlin + Jetpack Compose + Supabase

---

## 技术栈

| 层级     | 技术                            | 用途        |
| ------ | ----------------------------- | --------- |
| UI     | Jetpack Compose + Material3   | 声明式 UI    |
| 架构     | MVVM (ViewModel + Repository) | 业务逻辑分层    |
| DI     | Hilt                          | 依赖注入      |
| 本地存储   | Room                          | 状态缓存      |
| 后端     | Supabase REST API             | 数据同步      |
| 网络     | OkHttp                        | HTTP 客户端  |
| 小组件    | Glance                        | 桌面 Widget |
| 最低 SDK | Android 12 (API 31)           |           |
| 目标 SDK | Android 15 (API 35)           |           |

---

## 架构概览

```
app/
├── MainActivity.kt              # 入口 Activity + 庆祝动画集成
├── StandByApplication.kt        # Hilt 入口 + 设备 ID 初始化
├── data/
│   ├── local/                   # Room 本地缓存
│   │   ├── AppDatabase.kt
│   │   ├── StatusDao.kt
│   │   └── StatusEntity.kt
│   ├── model/                   # 数据模型
│   │   ├── Doing.kt             # 活动预设枚举（12 种）
│   │   ├── Feeling.kt           # 心情预设枚举（9 种 + 颜色）
│   │   ├── PairingInfo.kt       # 配对信息
│   │   └── UserStatus.kt        # 用户状态实体
│   ├── remote/                  # 网络层
│   │   ├── SupabaseConfig.kt    # Supabase 配置
│   │   └── SupabaseService.kt   # Supabase REST API 客户端
│   └── repository/              # 数据仓库
│       ├── PairingRepository.kt # 配对业务逻辑
│       └── StatusRepository.kt  # 状态业务逻辑
├── di/
│   ├── AppModule.kt             # Room 数据库提供
│   └── SupabaseModule.kt        # Supabase 服务提供
├── fcm/                         # [已删除] Firebase Cloud Messaging
├── navigation/
│   └── Routes.kt                # 导航路由定义
├── ui/
│   ├── celebration/             # 庆祝动画
│   │   ├── CelebrationDay.kt    # 纪念日数据类
│   │   ├── CelebrationConfig.kt # 纪念日配置列表
│   │   └── CelebrationOverlay.kt# 全屏粒子动画覆盖层
│   ├── components/              # 可复用组件
│   │   ├── AvatarWithGlow.kt    # 呼吸发光头像
│   │   ├── DoingPicker.kt       # 活动选择器
│   │   ├── FeelingPicker.kt     # 心情选择器（含颜色动画）
│   │   └── StatusCard.kt        # 状态卡片（渐变背景）
│   ├── home/                    # 首页
│   │   ├── HomeScreen.kt        # 对方卡片 + 我的状态 + 发布按钮
│   │   └── HomeViewModel.kt     # 状态轮询 + 伙伴查找
│   ├── history/                 # 历史记录
│   │   ├── HistoryScreen.kt     # 时间线列表
│   │   └── HistoryViewModel.kt  # 历史查询
│   ├── poststatus/              # 发布状态
│   │   ├── PostStatusScreen.kt  # 心情/活动选择 + 备注
│   │   └── PostStatusViewModel.kt
│   ├── settings/                # 设置/配对
│   │   ├── SettingsScreen.kt    # 配对 + 已绑定状态
│   │   └── SettingsViewModel.kt
│   └── theme/                   # 主题
│       ├──

...(output truncated for display)
```
